ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u1	Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. The meeting was resumed. Take your seats, please. We begin with the point corresponding to the validation or repeal of royal decrees-laws. First, there is the debate on the validation or repeal of Royal Decree-Law 17/2021 of 14 September of urgent measures to mitigate the impact of the price escalation of natural gas on retail gas and electricity markets. To present the Royal Decree-Law, the third Vice-President and Minister for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u2	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, good morning and thank you very much for your presence at this session, which aims in this first part to present to you for validation the Royal Decree-Law 17/2021 of 14 September of urgent measures to mitigate the impact of the price escalation of natural gas on retail gas and electricity markets; a decree-law which includes a set of temporary measures and structural measures to cushion the impact of the price on the wholesale market of natural gas on the final electricity and gas bill of consumers. We have witnessed a sudden, extraordinary, unprecedented rise in the price of gas in international markets, which has been rapidly and unexpectedly transmitted to the price of electricity in wholesale markets. It is a situation motivated by external factors undoubtedly, but to which the Government has an obligation to respond in favour of the general interest in order to prevent a decline in the competitiveness of our productive fabric, the deterioration of domestic economies or the disruption of the recovery, transformation and modernization of our productive systems. Let me remind you of some data to put into context and figures what we are talking about. The electricity price of the European wholesale markets, after marking a monthly minimum in February 2021, has recorded historical values never seen, unprecedented prices that happen week after week. The maximum point was the one marked on 7 October, a historical maximum daily of 288.53 euros the megawatt/hour in Spain, which, however, remained below other countries with higher values, as was the case in France with 298.3 euros megawatt/hour, Germany with 302.5 or Italy with 300.7 euros. The tension has even led the UK to live megawatt/hour prices of over £1400, China to order its industry to shut down 33% of hours, or India to take on power cuts for two or three hours. In the European case, we can see some circumstances associated with the design of the wholesale electricity market. In some ways it has been questioned by the huge cost differential of the different technologies, reflecting deorbited and sustained prices, however, for weeks, for months. The price of fossil fuels and their volatility generates an amplifying effect, which, sustained over time, makes the functioning of the market unfeasible as initially designed. The extraordinary increase maintained over time in the costs of a technology cannot be translated into the generalised increase in the price of all electricity, it cannot be assumed. It equals how much cheap energy there is in the system: the market marks the price of the most expensive. Our commitment as a government is to prevent this from having a sustained impact on consumers' bills. We know that every increase of one euro per megawatt hour in the cost of natural gas results in an increase in the price of electricity in the wholesale market of two euros per megawatt hour. It is true that what we are experiencing is largely due to a mismatch between supply and demand, in addition to other geopolitical factors. The lack of investment in gas infrastructure, the rapid recovery of global demand above the forecast after the pandemic, leading to a 35% growth in Chinese demand in the first half of the year in relation to its pre-pandemic demand, or the incidence of other geopolitical motivations have led to alarms among analysts, institutions, industry and governments. From the supply side, the hibernation of certain plants, the slowdown or even the reduction of investments in recent years has temporarily reduced the production and export capacity of natural gas, in addition to the smallest injection of natural gas from Russia to Europe. On the demand side, the faster than expected economic recovery, as indicated, has meant an increase in consumption above forecast. The truth is that Asia and Europe, with low levels of stocks to cope with winter, demand a volume of natural gas above previous years to minimize any supply risk in cold months. In other words, we find ourselves in a scenario in which supply does not correspond to demand, we all compete in the same market, promoting the quotation of natural gas upwards, and, as a result, we stress the price of electricity to the design of the electricity market; an unprecedented situation due to both its magnitude and its duration. Our conviction is that it is not feasible to maintain shortage prices for a long time, especially when the actual generation costs do not correspond to the increase in average system prices. Any recent forecasts regarding the evolution of gas prices, including the most adverse scenarios of analysts and international agencies just weeks or even days ago, have been out of date. It is therefore important to avoid or minimize the contagion of gas prices in electricity, as this would eventually be reflected in an increase in our goods and services and in the comfort of families. In this respect, we agree with the analysis made public yesterday by the European Commission. The impact of natural gas has an impact on the economy as a whole, especially on the most vulnerable groups and industry, which in our country, unlike in others, does not have sufficient long-term coverage. The mismatch between the supply and demand of natural gas and other raw materials on a global scale not only explains the tensions experienced, which I have already described, but also warns us of several things to which I have referred, but I want to summarize in a systematic way. Firstly, the September forecasts have been out of date; we do not know how long or how intense this situation will be, forcing us to review the measures taken and adapt them to the new circumstances. We trust that this will be temporary effects, but we must be prepared to deal with them. Secondly, the regulatory design of the electricity market has facilitated the amplification of the cost overrun of natural gas in the electricity price of the wholesale market. Thirdly, we are convinced that the most appropriate level of response is European, and that is why this is a matter that needs to be addressed with sufficient agility in order to be able to agree on exceptional responses to exceptional situations such as the current one. We are grateful for the proposals contained in yesterday's notification to the Commission on response measures. However, we are convinced that further work is needed. From Spain we have sent a number of proposals to the Community authorities. Firstly, reform the rules of the internal electricity market to prevent large cost differences between different generation technologies from burdening the final prices paid on the market. Secondly, limit the participation in the market of emission allowances to agents and operators subject to the obligation to deliver these rights and their representatives in order to avoid to the greatest extent possible distortions in the signal of CO2 prices motivated by speculative behaviour. Thirdly, to activate a centralised purchasing mechanism for natural gas and to create strategic gas reserves at European level. As I said, certainly our preference is the coordinated response at European level, obviously complemented by the necessary national measures. In parallel, it is necessary to update the mechanisms for governance and global coordination in the face of the simultaneous demand for the same goods in international markets. In any case, they have no doubt, speeding up the energy transition is the solution to this volatility. There is a need to adequately monitor the pace of supply and demand transformation. Against this backdrop, this Government continues to work to provide adequate responses to vulnerable families and consumers, to industry, particularly exposed to the price of electricity given its level of coverage, and to investors through the development of attractive investment frameworks that facilitate the acceleration of transition through the development of energy efficiency, electrification, distributed generation or penetration of renewable energy and storage projects in the system. This is not a novelty of this decree-law. In June, when the wholesale market set an average price of €83.3 megawatt/hour, far from current values, the government adopted a 10% VAT reduction for consumers with less than 10 kilowatts hired and the 7 % tax suspension per generation. These two measures reduced the price of electricity in the wholesale market in a cross-cutting way, so that the final bill for all domestic and industrial consumers felt a significant relief during the summer. In September, with the same commitment of June, we adopted the royal decree-law that I present today for validation. This is a shock plan in an extraordinary context with which we want to ensure that households, self-reliant, SMEs, industries and businesses can tackle economic recovery and, above all, we can protect the most vulnerable groups. In the coming weeks we hope to be able to complement it with additional measures to ensure coverage through bilateral contracts and reasonable prices prior to the spiral of natural gas for the industry, simultaneously reinforcing the position of vulnerable consumers receiving the social bonus. Allow me to briefly describe the measures included in this Decree-Law. Firstly, in the tax field, the 7 % tax suspension is extended during the fourth quarter of 2021 and the excise duty on electricity is reduced by 90 %, to the minimum permitted by the 2003 Directive establishing the Community system of taxation on energy products and electricity. The combination of these two measures, together with the temporary reduction of VAT and the injection into the electricity system of additional revenues linked to the revenues of CO emission auctions, represents an unprecedented fiscal effort: more than EUR 4 billion, an unprecedented figure from the public budget, to cover the electricity system. And to these measures of public spending are added others, such as the limitation of the rise of the regulated rate of natural gas during the last trimester of the year 2021 and the first trimester of 2022. The current regulation provides for the automatic quarterly review of tariffs based on the evolution of gas prices. This implies that the impact of the automatic review would have entailed an average increase in the last resort rate of 30 or 40 % compared to the update carried out in June 2021. Undoubtedly, this is something that is not easy to assume for any family, for any domestic consumer. This automatic increase would mean a huge economic effort for domestic consumers and SMEs precisely in the months in which gas is most demanded, during the autumn and winter months. That is why we have decided to adopt this temporary and exceptional measure, limiting in the next two quarters the increase in the tariff to an average maximum of 4.6 per quarter. On the other hand, the Decree-Law provides for the creation of long-term energy auctions with the intention of increasing the liquidity of the future electricity market and facilitating competition in the retail market, making it easier for these small traders to have access to electricity at reasonable prices other than those of the pool. One of the most important factors contributing to the lack of liquidity in future markets is the lack of incentive to participate in parallel to the marketing of electricity offered by vertically integrated groups. This lack of liquidity has a negative impact on competition. Independent marketers and large consumers have difficulty acquiring a significant volume of energy in the long term beyond a quarter without high premiums, holding back their investment decisions and putting their business plans at risk. That is why we believe it is appropriate to have a new instrument that will encourage entry and retain the development of companies with marketing activities, increasing competition and, therefore, seeking incentives, lower prices for the final consumer. Thirdly, the Decree-Law amends the Water Act to include criteria for the rational use of water resources. This reform specifies the criteria for the application of the regime laid down in Article 55.2 of the consolidated Water Act to ensure a rational use of the resource, compatible with its adaptation to the consequences of climate change and for the benefit of the environment and of all water users. For reservoirs greater than 50 cubic hectares of total capacity whose main uses are not the supply, irrigation and other agricultural uses, in cases where it is appropriate to attend to the reservoir of water and the seasonal prediction, the reform provides that the basin body must fix at the beginning of each hydrological year, on a proposal from the commission of the reservoir, a series of hydrological variables that maintain in an orderly manner the capacity of disassembly and turbine. In addition, the Decree-Law states that rational exploitation must be compatible with the development of sustainable economic activities linked to the dynamism of coastal municipalities, always within the framework of the order of preference for uses established in the relevant watershed hydrological plan. On the other hand, we create the concept of minimum vital supply. In line with the Energy Poverty Strategy and in accordance with our coalition commitment, with our government commitment, this royal decree-law creates the figure of the minimum vital supply, extending the four-month period provided for in the current legislation to ensure that vulnerable consumers do not see their supply interrupted at a minimum essential power to ensure comfort in homes. Perhaps the most mentioned exceptional measure in recent weeks has been to recover in favour of all consumers the extraordinary benefits that could have been obtained by electricity companies as a result of the natural gas price rally, a measure of a temporary character in the face of an aggravated situation over time regulating the ex-post reduction in the remuneration of non-emitting sub-marginal power stations up to 31 March for an amount proportional to the value of the contribution of gas on the Iberian market, excluding those receiving specific and small remuneration schemes. I want to make it clear, first of all, that it is a minority applicable only to those who have internalised those extraordinary benefits, not to those who have not; to those who have guaranteed stable prices before the escalation for those final consumers with whom they have hired him. And we do not want it to be for those who guarantee new stable prices that do not internalise the evolution of gas prices. On the contrary, we need to boost reasonable price coverage for our industry and that is why we are working to ensure its correct application and development. I am grateful for the efforts of all those who, from civil society, from the parliamentary side, from the industry from the very beginning, have helped to identify how to help in these exceptional circumstances. We will ensure above all that the consumer's interest, whether domestic or industrial, simultaneously favouring the acceleration of the energy transition, more investment in efficiency, distributed generation or electrification. I am sure that with the commitment and constructive will of all we will be able to mitigate the harmful effects of the evolution of the rise of natural gas quotation. Finally, in line with all the previous measures, the Decree-Law provides for the reduction of charges until 31 December 2021 by 97 %, thus instantly reducing the final bill to consumers. Beyond the specific content of the measures, as you may have had the opportunity to analyse and evaluate, I would like to emphasise the following aspects common to all of them: firstly, they are measures with a direct impact on the electricity and gas bill of the final consumers, aimed at counteracting the expected rising prices for this winter, with particular attention to vulnerable consumers; secondly, we identify those which are eminently temporary, extraordinary measures for an exceptional situation, and therefore different time horizons have been established in each case; and thirdly, they are measures that respect the principle of reasonable profitability, reliability and viability of new investments, i.e. measures with the triple objective of protecting consumers, ensuring that long-term contracts for industrial consumers are maintained and that new ones are closed at pre-spiral prices and developing an attractive regulatory framework for investments in energy transition, while last mentioning that our preference remains a coordinated and urgent action at European level to solve this problem alone, but rather laying the foundations of a current energy model in transition that responds to flexible and volatile resources. For this we will continue to work within the Community framework, for this we will continue to work within the national framework. Taking into account all the above, I request all groups to support the validation of this Royal Decree-Law 17/2021 of 14 September of urgent measures to mitigate the impact of the price escalation of natural gas on retail gas and electricity markets. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u15	Thank you, Mr. Sayas. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Finally, Mrs Vehí Cantenys has the floor for the same parliamentary group.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u16	Good morning. Good morning, Minister. For us this royal decree is a good step, a first step for the control of the situation. We know that the situation is not easy, that IBEX companies have even dared to threaten to shut down nuclear power plants and therefore seems to us to be a first step, but insufficient. In fact, your analysis of consumer price control does not seem to us to be sufficient. In fact, there are companies that are stopping production right now because they can't pay the bills for light and there are families that can't pay either. We wanted to talk about water today. You, in Article 10 of the Royal Decree, speak of the efficient use of water resources; in fact, you amend the Water Law, but we miss a fundamental element, and it is that you say nothing about hydroelectric power plants. In many villages in the Pyrenees, hydroelectric power stations should already be in public hands, in the hands of citizens, but they continue to be in the hands of companies, despite the fact that concessions in many cases have expired. Next week we will present a non-law proposal here on hydroelectric power stations and on the need for them to return to public and common hands; we hope to obtain from you the favorable vote, which is the vote that would extend this royal decree to the needs that citizens have. It is unthinkable that in the midst of the climate crisis hydropower companies make money instead of guaranteeing energy. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u19	Thank you very much, Mr. Matute. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] For the Basque Parliamentary Group, Mrs Sagastizabal Unzetabarrenetxea has the floor. [[EAJ-PNV]]
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u20	Egun on. Good morning. Vice-President, this decree contains measures aimed at reducing the bill on the tax side, on charges and even on tolls, but not on the price-fixing side, since the marginalist system is in Europe's hands. It is a system that was created when the fixed and variable costs of technologies were similar and should be reviewed today, because the costs of current technologies differ greatly from one another. In this regard, we must recognise your efforts, Vice-President, together with that of other countries, to open up the European Union to this possibility, even in the long term. Having said that, we are also concerned about the measure concerning the reduction of the excessive remuneration associated with the price of gas, not because of the reduction itself, because we have always said that the transition has a cost and that we must pay it among all - consumers, economic and industrial sectors - and help those who need it most in this process, but because of the impact this measure has on industry at a time when it is trying to get out of the pandemic crisis. It is a measure which mainly affects large electricity companies, but which, as the current decree states, also affects producers of renewables that are financed with PPPs or with closed roofs and that has had to be clarified in order not to affect this minority, an interpretative rectification that is collected with clamps. I have been hearing for several days with amazement that the debate only revolves around positioning itself with or against electrics, as if everything else does not matter, forgetting the repercussions on the industry, forgetting about equity, because these measures, although positive, help almost equal all domestic consumers with different incomes and even benefit more those who have a light contract with a fixed price - those who do not affect the rise of the pool - than those of the PVPC; not forgetting also the existence of instruments used to stabilize prices, we forget that, without wanting to do so, the price of the industry will be increased. The debate goes much further, because the underlying problem is still there, which is nothing other than the electricity market, a market that does not work as well as it should. In this sense, as our spokesman said yesterday, it must be ensured that companies that have a diversified generation mix, with all kinds of technologies, do not play with them by putting them in place or making them to achieve greater profit or that even, with the revenues of that mix, they could possibly modify market prices. By the way, a public or private enterprise cannot lower prices if it has only one technology, if it is monoculture. We understand that it may be reasonable to want to deduct certain benefits for reasons of general interest in an exceptional situation, but I believe that, driven by the need to try to lower the invoice, we have not taken into account the existence of all the type of bilateral contracts or PPP that the industry uses to have a competitive and stable price. We have also assumed that all generators and marketers receive the pool price, when this is not the case, because not all the energy sold refers to the wholesale market price. If this measure is not modulated or corrected, what is going to happen - and in fact is happening - is the opposite effect of what is intended, that this additional cost for the large ones is passed on to the companies, to the industry, which have signed stable prices and is worse the remedy than the disease. It is not a question of giving in, and there is no reduction, but rather of modulating, correcting the measure so as not to harm those who already have a fixed price through a PPP. Nor is it acceptable for electric companies to try to use their own interests to defend their own interests, but the reality is clumsy and all of us believe that we are aware that it is the companies that will ultimately be affected by this proposed reduction, in addition to a context in which we have a fragile economy, recovering from a pandemic, and with companies with still tight margins. If we join this with the supply crisis, we'll finish up. We already have the first warnings: halting Sidenor's production in Euskadi. Is this what we want to get? Do we want to put the industry at risk? This measure can serve in the short term to reduce the charges of the invoice, but it can be bread for today and hunger for tomorrow, because the electricity market is the same, they are the same rules of price fixing, a marginalist system, where the price of balance will continue to mark the opportunity costs of the generation. However, if the problem is the exercise of market power on the part of the big ones, let us take steps in this direction, let us make the future market more liquid, let us negotiate more volume, so that all the traders can access it, we can close positions giving rise to much greater competition and a certain price construction, because what is clear is that the big companies, when carrying out the price coverage between them, do not have incentives to go to these markets. In this regard, we certainly appreciate that the decree provides for the promotion of long-term procurement through auctions. And as long as there is no redesign of the marginalist system, the market for term contracts, PPPs, in a more transparent, physical, financial way, should also be encouraged to achieve price stability, as well as to develop capacity mechanisms. We are aware of what this decree implies in positive terms, especially at the level of domestic consumers. We have no doubt that it will go ahead, but my group understands that we cannot afford to overburden the industry. The easy thing would be to make a speech in black and white - we just heard it - without nuances and vote without worrying about the concrete consequences. It's not our style. Consumers must be protected, but also industry must be protected because it is essential if we are to ensure economic recovery, investment and employment. If anything has taught us the pandemic, it is the need for a strong and resilient industry. We cannot afford decisions that further undermine their competitiveness and, of course, threaten their viability. Although we also know that if they come wrong, some will stand in front of companies with banners. This is not simply a reduction of some profits to electricity or a public enterprise, or that by lowering taxes or passing charges to the State General Budgets the high prices will be solved by a plumage and the problems of the electricity market will be solved. I wish it were that way! But it's not. This energy shock has shown us that there are no easy solutions to complex problems, let alone in the electricity market, and has demonstrated the need for all of us to understand very well the functioning of energy markets in order to carry out any kind of reform. Therefore, we believe that in the case before us and since the Government has opened up the possibility of clarifying, modulating or correcting this decree, this correction must come from the dialogue and agreement between the Government and the companies concerned: how much to minimize, to whom to minimize, how to divide the minority among the most vulnerable consumers, etc. This is the way and on it, minister, you will certainly be able to count on our collaboration. For all these reasons, we will abstain in the validation of the decree and vote not on its processing as a bill. Thank you very much.

For example, if producing a megawatt/hydro hour costs between 3 and 8 euros, when the megawatt/hour of the gas plants is 200 euros, the hydroelectric plants receive an overpayment of more than 2500 %. [[File number 130/000058]]
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u50	We turn to the next item: Validation or repeal of Royal Decree-Law 18/2021 of 28 September of urgent measures for the protection of employment, economic recovery and improvement of the labour market. To present the royal decree-law, I have the floor, on behalf of the Government, the second Vice-President and Minister of Labour and Social Economy.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u51	Thank you. Good morning. I compare with you to outline the content and scope of Royal Decree-Law 18/2021 of 28 September, which constitutes a further extension of the exceptional mechanism of ERTE articulated by this Government during the pandemic. It is, ladies and gentlemen, as you know, the sixth social agreement in defence of employment, signed hand in hand with the social agents of our country and the expression of a desire for dialogue and meeting that is not circumstantial, but is projected in each and every one of our decisions. It is not necessary to remind them of the exceptionality and urgency of this rule, nor of the fact that the protection deployed by the ERTEs in the acute phase of the pandemic remains necessary to continue to protect companies and workers in certain sectors and activities, as they have done so far. Let me remind you of some figures which, I sincerely believe, are very eloquent. The ERTEs, which are decisive in the rapid and intense recovery of activity and employment, have led to the fastest recovery in our history. Faced with the ten years and eleven months of the financial crisis, this time in fifteen months we have returned to levels of social security membership prior to the crisis. ERTEs have ruled out employment as an adjustment variable, avoided layoffs, business closures and permanent damage to the productive fabric. That's why recovery is faster now. In line with the European response, we have opted for an internal flexibility mechanism agreed between businesses and trade unions. At the harshest time of the crisis, in the phase of confinement, the ERTE protected more than 550,000 companies and 3,617,000 workers, a shared effort by trade unions, businesses, workers, the Government and this country. By April 2020, 24% of wage earners were covered by an ERTE, one in four. Today it barely reaches 1%. This is the measure of the success of the ERTEs. There are only 230,000 regulated people left, of whom one third have a partial suspension, that is, they are working part of their day. In this year and a half, almost three and a half million people have returned to work and have done so in keeping with all their rights. The ones that follow at ERTE are people who work in very localized activities, concentrated in tourism, hotels, air transport or travel agencies, who, as you know, have not yet regained full normality. Protecting people is not only a matter of social justice, we have also shown that it is the most efficient way to deal with a crisis. The evidence of these data that I have presented, ladies and gentlemen, shows us that the ERTE system has worked, which has the backing of reality, in the same way as another measure, such as the rise in the interprofessional minimum wage, was validated this week by the Nobel Prize in Economics, scientifically demonstrating that such an increase does not result in more unemployment. The protection deployed by this royal decree takes into account all these figures and evidences and also acquires a new face, far from a health emergency that, with great collective effort, we are leaving behind. The ERTs that are defined here are new generation ERTs, a bridge that anticipates this structural and solid response to which we have committed ourselves to Europe, in which training will take on a decisive and transformative role. We are at the same time laying the foundations for a new business and work model that is already pushing us out of the crisis, but which will also arrive at a healthier socio-economic context, more respectful of the rights of working people. The proof that without cuts or austerity we were able to grow efficiently. The rules are composed of objective precepts, it is also, but there is an argumental, intangible and valuable element that underlies this whole articulated: tranquility, certainty, the effective response that comes from social dialogue to reach every home, every family, every company, the workers of our country. It would not have been possible without the help of the social partners, as I have already mentioned so many times, and it would not have been possible without the groups that have understood these measures and have accompanied the Government always in this House. Once again, as I say, I want to thank you for your high-mindedness and your involvement in the search for collective solutions. That same concern, that desire to give tranquillity and certainty is what has urged us in this royal decree to articulate protective measures for the working people, for the enterprises, for the autonomous people, also for those of the island of La Palma, affected by the emergency caused by the eruption of the volcano of Old Summit, but also for the whole of the Canary Islands, conscious as we are of its economic and labor singularity. I begin to detail briefly the main features of this royal decree. The Royal Decree-Law 18/2021 maintains the centrality of ERTE as a priority coverage system until 28 February next year. It is a period that leads to this calm that I have been talking about and that already leaves us at the door of the holiday period of Holy Week, an annual milestone, as you know, of the revival of tourism activity in our country and, in parallel, of economic activity and employment. The 31st of October is established as a transitional bridge date for the ERTE which until now were automatic renewal. Companies will therefore have the deadline this month to overcome those emergency ERTs born from the initial Royal Decree-Law 8/2020 and join this new modality that places training as a priority mechanism of sustainability in employment. The ETREs of impediment and limitation will remain linked to force majeure, with similar scope to those of the previous royal decrees, and the simplification of procedures for moving from an ETRE of impediment to one of limitation and vice versa is also maintained. I would like to dwell in particular on the formative nature of this new generation of ERTE, which aligns itself with the legislation of those countries that support companies from within, from their own capacity, to create confidence and also stability. This efficient and future-sized training is the cornerstone of this royal decree-law, because it is the gateway to social security exemptions that distinguish and recognize this corporate commitment. Thus, Royal Decree-Law 18/2021 has established mechanisms to encourage training by companies. It does so primarily by significantly increasing training credit, setting a significantly higher amount for small businesses, which are traditionally the furthest away from this mechanism. The exemptions are designed, in short, to encourage training without penalizing small businesses and trying to attract them to a system that has not so far attended. We are sensitive to this reality and we are aware of the need to integrate these small enterprises, which form the basis of the Spanish economy, into the training dynamics that are being generated. The training that allows for increased exemptions is therefore, as you know, both the subsidized and the subsidized. And he announced that on the latter the government will make special calls in the coming weeks for people working at ERTE. This royal decree maintains the already known limits and conditions that relate to corporate social responsibility. They are limits and conditions consistent with the high public investment involved in the application of the ERTE and which, as you know, oblige the maintenance of employment, establish the prohibition of the distribution of dividends, shape the need for fiscal transparency, determine the impossibility of performing overtime, prevent the practice of outsourcing or new hirings with identical professional categories to those existing in the companies. Of particular relevance is the extension of the popularly termed 'zero meter' that extends throughout the year 2022. Workers who receive a benefit during an ERTE will be able to keep intact their accumulated unemployment benefit throughout the next year. That is also conducive to peace of mind and certainty. The 70% guarantee of the regulatory base is also maintained; that is, we are not applying the previous legislation designed by the People's Party. They are measures that have proved essential during the pandemic and are now involved in the new recovery phase by generating that confidence in the future that is so necessary for life and for our projects. To this same end, in the present royal decree-law the same benefits are maintained in the provision of cessation of activity of the autonomous persons, who during the pandemic, as you know, have allowed the professionals and small entrepreneurs to survive their projects. We thus set the necessary adaptations to effectively accompany the autonomous people in the return to their activity and in the reconfiguration of their business initiatives. It was also essential to maintain special coverage for discontinuous fixed persons engaged in regular fixed work. It is also necessary to maintain the special unemployment coverage of artists from all over the world of culture, of those who carry out their activities in the performing arts and public shows, which also extends until 28 February 2022. I would also like to point out this Government's commitment to the structural and future improvement of the living and working conditions of these groups. For this reason, as you know, the elaboration of the artist's status is progressing at a good pace and so the fixed discontinuous, rights and guarantees will be an essential part of the modernization of our labour relations system, as is being proposed at the Social Dialogue Bureau, which, as you know, held a new meeting yesterday. I would also like to highlight the extension of the Care Plan until next 28 February in this Royal Decree-Law. The maintenance of this plan, which reinforces the right to adapt work to people, has a very special meaning. We hope that care will play a central and structural role in the business dynamics of the future, as will ERTE itself. I would like to conclude by making special reference to the measures taken in this Royal Decree-Law to address the exceptional situation of La Palma's enterprises, workers and self-employed persons. It is a specific ARTE that contemplates the emergency situation caused by the volcanic eruption of Cumbre Vieja and that also values the labour and economic singularity of the Canarian community. We will accompany palm trees and palm trees with everything in our hand as long as necessary, despite the fact that there is a first deadline until 28 February. In fact, a total of sixty-five companies in La Palma have already hosted these specific ERTEs, currently protecting 514 workers. We are aware of the impact that this situation will unfortunately have on all businesses, the economy and work on the island. We have also just approved the extraordinary employment plan for the island of La Palma, which amounts to EUR 63 million. It is a whole coverage that will be adapted at all times and immediately, as the present royal decree-law does, to the evolution of the situation. I have already asked your ladies and gentlemen to validate this royal decree-law which extends the ERTE until 28 February 2022 and which places the formation at the epicenter of our effective response to the recovery process that we are now facing as a country. I am talking to you about training throughout the life cycle, which is solid and adapts to change and which involves companies daily in the response to crises and transformations. I am talking about investing in scientific and technical innovation, in expert knowledge, and in the end, in qualification. All of this must be among our priorities. I speak to them of social equality, of strengthening the link of all working people, and especially of our youth, with the welfare state, with measures such as those that include this royal decree-law, whose title is clear in its objectives: the protection of employment, economic recovery and the improvement of the labour market. I am speaking to you, as I said at the beginning of my speech, about calm and certainties, which are so necessary at the moment. I speak to you about what Hannah Arendt taught us, the right to have rights; this is democracy. And I ask you, with your vote, to support this new step towards a recovery that must be fair in order for rules like this to be a decisive lever. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u77	We continue with the item on the agenda for the debate on the whole of the bill dealing with the remuneration of unissued CO2 from the electricity market. For the introduction of the bill, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the third Vice-President and Minister for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u78	President, ladies and gentlemen, I am addressing you today to defend the draft law on unissued CO2 compensation from the electricity market and, therefore, to ask you to support it and to vote against the amendments to the whole presented. And I have to say that I have listened carefully to Mr Movellán's speech, as concerned as his group says it is about the consumer light bill, and yet with the light with which they vote against - as they have always done - the reduction of taxation, the reduction of the cost and the impact of the wholesale market on the Spanish bill. Surprisingly, an exercise of coherence proper to those who have been practicing this opposition, and therefore it is not surprising that they do the same with this bill. This instrument that we are discussing today, together with the creation of the national fund for the sustainability of the electricity system, helps to reduce the electricity bill of families, industries and SMEs, accelerate the energy transition and promote the necessary electrification for decarbonisation. The CO2 reduction mechanism designed by the law seeks to recover part of the infra-marginal income attributable to the carbon dividend in favour of all consumers and seeks to facilitate the participation of the whole system in the dividends generated by the CO2 price. As you know - and we have been talking about this this morning - the Spanish electricity market, like that of the other members of the European Union and the vast majority of OECD countries, is a marginal market, in which the latest technology covering demand is the one that determines the price for the rest of the technologies. The most common thing is that this price is marked in the wholesale market by CO2-emitting installations, and therefore a part of the final price paid to all generators corresponds to these emission allowances, which pay those who must comply with the polluter-pays principle and receive those who do not issue or have to report for it. This is a cost that also pays for all infra-marginal technologies, and it is about this additional remuneration that has internalised the cost of CO2 emissions allowances on which the law acts. The recovery of part of the income does not alter at all the regulatory framework for price fixing designed in Europe; the marginal market will continue to operate and determine the price and order of sale on the market, to which all electricity is remunerated through the usual supply and demand check, as laid down in Articles 3 and 7.2 of the European Union Regulation 2019/943. Emissions trading has been enormously important, it is an engine of change in the investment decisions of companies, it has allowed since its start more than fifteen years ago adequate signals to drastically reduce emissions in electricity generation and encourage investment in renewables, becoming today the lowest operating cost technologies, the most present in our energy system. Ladies and gentlemen, this proposal seeks to recover part of the dividend, as I told you, which has received non-broadcasting facilities in operation since before 2003 in favour of the system as a whole, and stressed before 2003 because many of the non-broadcasting plants have been operating since before Directive 2003/87, which created the European Union's emission allowance market. Therefore, when they decided at the time to address the necessary investments, they could not take into account the impact of the European system on their investment and profitability calculations. As I said, it is a proposal compatible with the European framework, consistent with the principles of the internal electricity market, the carbon market in the European Union, legal certainty and the legitimate expectations of investors. It is a proposal similar to that applied between 2006 and 2009, which was expressly validated by the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 17 October 2013 and by the Supreme Court on 1 April 2014. Both the Supreme Court and the Court of Justice of the European Union stated that the marginalist system of the electricity market generates'situations of - I quote literally - undeserved gains in so far as the internalisation of the value of emission allowances and the cost structure are reflected in the price of electricity received by all electricity producers active in the wholesale electricity market'. The Act also incorporates the recommendations of the Council of State's opinion of 21 July 2021, strengthening legal certainty and the principles of reasonable profitability, legitimate confidence and proportionality. According to that opinion, this is a solution that is respectful of the principle of legal certainty, which adequately protects the legitimate expectations of operators, but prevents any rise in emission allowances from benefiting those infra-marginal technologies that do not incur emission costs, thereby protecting consumers from rising electricity prices. Our Government is committed to consumer protection. We believe that the changes introduced after the opinion of the Council of State reinforce the situation of consumers, also reinforce the situation of electricity companies. What are those changes with respect to the initial version? First, the establishment of a floor price of 20,7 euros/tonne, which coincides with the average quote of allowances on the market from 2017 until the time when the preliminary draft law was adopted. In this way, the recommendation is incorporated that the amount of the reduction be linked to the difference between the price of allowances at each time and an average price, a market reference in recent years. Thus, trust in investment is not linked to the time of initial investment, but to the average value of rights in recent years. On the other hand, we advance the reference date for the application of the reduction to the facilities concerned. At first we had introduced the date of 2005, because it is the year in which the European system comes into force in our country. Faced with this, we incorporated the year 2003, when this directive was adopted and published in the Official Journal of the European Union, thereby reinforcing the principle of legitimate confidence. In this regard, the Council of State affirms that this progress has logic from the perspective of the principle of legal certainty and the protection of legitimate confidence insofar as it excludes from the minority all those operators who invested after that date, 25 October 2003, which could already have the economic expectation of profits, as a result of the emissions trading regime and have internalized this value in their accounts when making the investment calculations. The Council of State, ladies and gentlemen, has no doubt supported this mechanism in its opinion. The planned mechanism for reducing the over-income received - the High Council points out - is suitable both for the purpose pursued, to reduce the price of electricity and to promote the electrification of the economy, as well as for the general lines of its design and justification. The opinion confirms that the law is in accordance with European Union law and the criterion of reasonable profitability and argues that the marginalist nature of the market does not mean that interventions cannot take place, provided that they are justified and are not discriminatory, as is the case here. With regard to the development of the law, I would like to point out that the non-stationary power stations that came into operation after 2003, as I have indicated, are excluded from the minority. It therefore does not apply to investment in new renewable plants, which is key to the decarbonisation process of our energy mix and to the achievement of the climate objectives we have set as a country. It shall not affect this mechanism either to non-stationary power stations which are hosted by any regulated remuneration framework or to installations less than 10 megawatts or located in non-penisular territory electrical systems, regardless of their date of commissioning. The annual revenue received by this reduction will be intended to finance capacity mechanisms and charges in the electricity system, among other things, which currently fall on consumers, thereby effectively reducing the bill for domestic consumers, SMEs and industries. In particular, they will be aimed at financing renewable energy charges, encouraging the sharing of the costs of the transition between all technologies and giving great impetus to electrification. Our estimate is that this law, together with the contribution of the national fund for the sustainability of the electricity system, will help reduce the bill of domestic consumers to 15%, almost 14% for SMEs or 5.22% for large consumers. In addition, it contributes to the special protection of the most vulnerable groups by establishing that 10% of the revenue is to be used to co-finance the costs of public administrations related to severely vulnerable consumers at risk of social exclusion. As for the amendment to the whole tabled by the People's Party, ladies and gentlemen, I said before that I am not surprised, although I do not resist making a small comment. I am not surprised by those who have not supported any of the measures for the energy transition and left their duties unanswered, I am not surprised by those who have made it difficult and left behind a legacy of conflict and demands, I am not surprised by those who have voted against the coverage of coal miners and workers, I am not surprised by those who announce their vote against the tax reduction measures and the fixed costs of the invoice, what exactly they air and what they devote great efforts to, as if it were their solution. It is difficult to understand how one can change one's mind in such a limited amount of time, but also in this case it was you, ladies and gentlemen of the People's Party, who on 26 May of this same year, not a week before the preliminary draft minority law will be published for public consultation, who submitted an amendment to the Royal Decree Law 34/2020 requesting the creation of a similar mechanism. And I quote: 'The Ministry of Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge will develop within three months a mechanism of the limitation of the overpayment that non-carbon emission technologies can perceive as a result of the incorporation of CO2 into wholesale market prices by the emission technologies'. I do not know whether you have repented of your own proposals or whether, as seems to be usual, you practice the policy of systematically going against everything that is said at any time with regard to any circumstance whatsoever. Ladies and gentlemen, this measure that I advocate today, along with the other structural measures that the Government has been taking for three years and the joint action of citizens and the private sector, will enable us to facilitate the energy transition in a fair and equitable way, sharing costs, facilitating access to profits and dividends and, above all, protecting the most vulnerable groups. I therefore call on you to support a measure which is highly demanded socially and politically, respectfully, legally guaranteed and proportionate to the objective pursued and which is within the framework of the rules of the national and European market and does not distort price signals. Our commitment is to tackle the energy transition in the best conditions, in the fairest way and protecting consumers in particular. I trust that this responsibility can be shared and supported by all of us in this House. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u79	Thank you very much. An amendment to the entire alternative text has been submitted to this bill. In defence of the amendment tabled, the People's Parliamentary Group in Congress, Mr Gago Bugarín, has the floor. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]]

On the other hand, inefficiency in management because none, absolutely none of the measures that they have adopted without dialogue with the main opposition party has managed to reduce the high aggression of our country's electricity tariff. The rate rose by 245 % in May with the Climate Change Act; it rose by 252 % in June with the approval of the preliminary draft law of the National Fund for the Sustainability of the Electricity System; it rose by 290 % in August with the approval of the preliminary draft law that we are currently debating; it rose by 350 % in September with the approval of the Royal Decree-Law 17/2021, debated this morning, and today the price of light stands at 215 euros per hour, compared to 40 euros that cost the same day a year ago. Externalities yes, Mrs Ribera, but the lack of anticipation and inefficiency in management, too. An ideological bias that has led him to raise the price of diesel to eighteen million Spaniards, while eliminating the Renove Plan and while we have the oldest car park in Europe. The same ideological bias that has led you also to change the hourly pricing of SMEs and small consumers to increase the hourly range, precisely when there is more demand. I mean, it's taken him to tell the Spaniards to do at 12 o'clock in the evening what they were doing at 8 o'clock in the afternoon. And an ideological bias because Royal Decree-Law 17/2021 and this bill that seeks to impose a tax on water, wind and nuclear clearly undermines the incorporation, deployment of renewables, the reduction of CO2 and the competitiveness of national industry. Therefore, Mrs Rivera, this is not a fair transition; this is a forced, erratic and painful conversion for the Spanish as a whole. The European Commission literally called it an interventionist. Yesterday, a uniform from the European Commission states, with reference to the new Spanish energy measures, that decisions that affect the sustainability of the marginal market cannot be made. The Council of State and the National Commission on Markets and Competition asked him to leave the wind industry without affecting this bill by the principle of rebuttableness. The main rating agents, for example, Fitch, Moody's or Standard and Poor's, among others, published the following assessments: one, the measures demonstrate the degree of political interference in Spain; two, there is uncertainty as to whether other interventionist measures are to be replicated in the future; three, the companies need investment cuts to protect risk ratings; four, the companies had almost all their base generation for 2021 and 2022 at significantly lower prices than the current spot market, so the measure results in a loss for generators and consumers, and five, the measures taken could threaten the 2019 nuclear agreement between operators and the State, the proposed limit in the price mechanism will make nuclear plants not profitable. However, it ceases to do so where it can, through sincere negotiations with the nuclear and hydroelectric installations sector, provide a stable medium- and long-term remuneration and regulatory framework that will ultimately allow the necessary investments to increase useful life, to increase pumping capacity, to increase and create reserve, capacity and hybridization mechanisms, in short, to mitigate volatility and peaks on the wholesale market in the short term. We are therefore faced with initiatives that will bring decisive structural changes and that will benefit all and, in particular, the most vulnerable. In short, these are measures, which you propose, in which only the State assumes the burden of the cost of reducing the price of light. It draws attention to the fact that an amendment to the whole of this bill is being tabled, which seeks to ensure that part of the burden of measures to reduce the price of light falls on the electricity companies themselves. The whole effort cannot be done by the state as long as the electrics get far too many benefits. No one should be surprised, therefore, that we do not ask for empathy from the electricians, but that we share with them the burden of the cost of light with a small part of those benefits. ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u106	Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. I rise to the rostrum to present the democratic memory bill which, as you know, adopted by the Council of Ministers on 20 July. I also take this speaking time to thank my predecessor's work in the ministry, Carmen Calvo, who devoted so much effort and so much work to this project. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, objects have memory. I am not referring only to the monuments and museums that keep the memory of societies, of civilizations, of times; I am referring above all to the objects that condense the memory of a person or of a family. Two years ago, the excavation team that was investigating a pit in Cevico de la Torre, Palencia, found a surprising object: a rattlesnake. The researchers were surprised that an object so linked to joy and life was found in a place so marked by hatred and pain, and wanted to know the history of that object. That rattlesnake turned out to be the last thing Catalina Muñoz took when she was taken from her house; a house she never returned to. It belonged to his son Martín, who in 1936 was nine months old. The memory of objects, ladies and gentlemen, is so powerful that in just a moment it can evoke whole lives. You can return us, even for a moment, to the people who are no longer there. Martin was never able to meet his mother, but he recovered that rattlesnake, eighty-three years later, which allowed him to meet her again. It allowed him to rescue her, as if the memory contained in her sonager was the intimate form of justice and reparation in her case. In Spain we have the fortune of having filmmakers who have reflected the history of thousands of people and families hit by the Civil War and the dictatorship. Recently Pedro Almodóvar has taken to the cinema precisely the story that I have just told them; Parallel Mothers is the film - a few days ago I could see it in Barcelona - that tells the story that I have just told them. It has an impressive realism the need that many families and people have to recover the remains of their loved ones. Unfortunately, this case is not exceptional, it is very common. While we are here debating, there are so many people in your house watching an object or photograph that contains a contradictory memory: they evoke an irreparable loss and, at the same time, they keep people to whom they remember safely from forgetting. Our duty, ladies and gentlemen, is that that evocative and restorative power that has memories also extends to the laws. That is natural and necessary in an advanced democracy like ours and that is the ultimate goal of this law, designed especially for people. The draft law on democratic memory presented to you is based on three pillars that make sense and make it possible to understand in its full dimension the objectives and the importance of their regulation. First, the project is based on democratic values: harmony, coexistence, political pluralism, the defence of human rights, equality between men and women, and the culture of peace. Secondly, it is anchored in the four fundamental principles of international humanitarian law: truth, justice, reparation and duty of memory as a guarantee of non-repetition. Moreover, with this law Spain will be located where, with the countries of our environment, the countries that have suffered dictatorships like ours and that have homologable laws to which I present you all today. [[Applauds]] It is a law that puts the victims, the victims of the 1936 coup d'état, the victims of the Civil War and the victims of the dictatorship, at the centre of all the victims; victims of different ideologies that suffered that black stage of our history. This law keeps his memory alive and seeks to morally repair his families. We are therefore taking a step further towards the improvement of our democracy. This is recognized by the statement of reasons for the rule, which proclaims that the conquest and consolidation of our constitutional framework has been the most significant historical achievement of Spanish society and defines the Transition as the basis of the era of greater splendor and greater prosperity that our country has experienced. The commitment of this law to the values of the Transition and the Constitution is indisputable, and that is precisely why we must continue to deepen those values. We need to update that commitment, and we need to do so with the legal possibilities of every time. Our transition was exemplary, it was done in an orderly manner in a very concrete historical, political and social context, but if we want to maintain the value and prestige of that process, we are now obliged to align with current international standards. It is necessary to promote and strengthen democratic memory policies in order to neutralize the forgetfulness and avoid the repetition of the most tragic episodes that our country has suffered. With the advent of democracy, the process of reparation, justice and forgiveness began. However, with the turn of the century, the memorial movement revealed the reality of thousands of Spanish families who continue to live and suffer in silence; a reality that had not been among the priorities of the first stage of our democracy. The Historical Memory Act of 2007 was one before and one after, addressed for the first time public policies in the field of democratic memory and gave a first democratic response to that void. The project that we are now presenting gives continuity to that law, and it does so by introducing improvements that are necessary. It responds to the requests of the memorialist associations, includes the recommendations of the international human rights agencies and also captures the contributions of the autonomous norms and political groups that we have received in recent years. I propose, ladies and gentlemen, that we work together on a text that is new, that is not a modification of the previous law, and that therefore allows us to build among all. It is a text with which we want to take a step forward, with which we want to settle a debt of democracy with our past and promote a discourse based on the defence of peace, pluralism and the condemnation of all forms of political totalitarianism. We too often hear that any initiative linked to the memory reopens wounds, and I have to tell you that I think it is exactly the opposite. It is a law that seeks to close all the wounds once and for all, that tries to heal the pain that still drags many people who suffered the war or repression of the dictatorship or suffered it from their parents, grandparents, uncles, relatives. It is about calming down the damage that silence caused to thousands of broken families. We want to end the uncertainty about the whereabouts of loved ones or simply restore their honor. It is also about opening the files to document hundreds of personal episodes that are known, but what legal limitations of access have prevented them from being documented. The memory for this Government is a state policy, and all political forces, all of them, should understand that condemning the dictatorship and honouring the victims is not ideology, it is justice, it is democracy. [[Applauds]] Today, the events that triggered a war that marked the destiny of the twentieth century in Spain are indisputable. That is why we put for the first time in a law the condemnation and repudiation of the coup d'état of 18 July 1936. [[Applauds]] It will be a way of reaffirming our democracy and of ceasing to be an anomaly in the European countries in our environment, because for many years now countries in our environment which have also suffered dictatorships, which have suffered Nazism, which have suffered fascism, have laws which we are presenting today in this House. It is also an indisputable fact, ladies and gentlemen, that Spain is still the country of Europe with the most missing people buried in mass graves, and when the remains are exhumanized and returned to their families, many people tell us that they can now die peacefully. That was the case with Ascension Mendieta. His father's only crime was to be the leader of UGT in his village. They took him away and he never came home. Ascension lived decades of silence waiting and before he died he had the fortune to fulfill the desire to recover his father's remains. However, ladies and gentlemen, there are compatriots who continue to wait for that moment, who have been suffering for eight decades, who have been waiting for it for eighty years because they have not been able to give a decent burial to their loved ones, and that suffering, that uncertainty has often also moved their descendants, their children, their nephews, their grandchildren. The State has to help them more than it has done so far, without any doubt. Another undisputed issue is the oblivion of the role of women in war and in the dictatorship. Therefore, the Government's proposal in this bill is to devote specific attention to women, who occupy a cross-cutting and very unique place throughout the law. Women played a very prominent role as active agents in the conquest of rights and freedoms that we all enjoy today. The law also provides for recognition of the double and specific repression suffered by many women simply and plainly by being so. [[Applauds]] Similarly, the project recognizes and strengthens the active role of the memorial movement in keeping the memory alive on the public agenda and on the social agenda of our country. It is also indisputable - we understand this from the government and I am sure that the democratic forces of this House will also understand - the incompatibility of democracy with the exaltation of the 1936 coup and the post-war dictatorship. It is no longer possible in public space to exalt its leaders or to discredit, humiliate or scorn the victims of war and dictatorship. It is also indisputable that the remains of the 1936 coupts cannot continue in pre-eminent places of public access other than cemeteries. [[Applauds]] An advanced democracy like ours cannot be allowed acts of exaltation or exaltation of human rights violations committed during the war or during the dictatorship. It is also indisputable to consider that all those who one day discovered that being babies were abducted and adopted without the free consent of their biological parents. [[Applauds]] Put yourself on your skin for a moment and tell us if we don't have the obligation to do something with all those victims of the dictatorship as well. These and many other situations deserve and justify a law that honors victims and meets their demands according to commonly accepted international parameters. I would like to point out, as you can imagine, that this is a very thoughtful and hard-working project, with great historical and legal rigour and with a fundamental role of the Secretariat of State for Democratic Memory, but it is also a project flexible enough to accept the amendments and incorporate the improvements that you all have to incorporate and propose during the parliamentary process. He then transferred some of the most relevant aspects of the law that I present to you. First, it highlights the express assumption of State leadership in the search for and identification of missing persons and the impulse to exhumations, responding to the legitimate demands of families that are not yet aware of the whereabouts of the remains of their loved ones. This search must be carried out by the General Administration of the State, without prejudice to the powers of other administrations. It is a collaborative process of collective dignity. Our country can no longer afford to have unworthy burials in its coves. [[Applauds]] Who can oppose locating, identifying and handing over the remains of a victim to their family members? Who can object to a daughter recovering the remains of her loved ones, her grandparents? It is not, ladies and gentlemen, a question of ideology, it is a question of justice, a question of democracy, a question of humanity and reparation. [[Applauds]] The law also provides for the creation of resources and institutions of indisputable value for their purposes. The Office of the Public Prosecutor for Human Rights and Democratic Memory is established in the Office of the Attorney General of the State, with different functions of investigation, coordination and conduct of investigations. A national DNA bank of victims of war and dictatorship is established. A register and census of victims is established, responding to the fragmentation and dispersion of information that we currently have available and creating a stable structure for the participation of the memorial movement through the council of democratic memory. We created two official days of memory, on 31 October, for the memory and tribute to all the victims, and on 8 May, for the memory and tribute of the people who went into exile. I want to tell you that the law does not differentiate between victims, I want to tell you that it does not discriminate against one another, I want to tell you that what we want is for all the victims of the dictatorship, all the victims of the war, all the victims of the coup to feel that this law honors them. We created a public census of all the victims of the war and also of all those who died in combat during the war. [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u107	Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u108	It includes a thorough regulation of the alleged anti-democratic memory, be it symbols, places, decorations, noble titles, associations or foundations that still advocate Francoism today. In addition, with the law we make significant progress in the documentary heritage, in order to protect documents and facilitate access to these funds and public archives; we address the declaration and regulation of commemorative functions of the places of memory, and, finally, we modify the regime of the monumental complex of the Valley of the Fallen as a step before their resignation. All these predictions are articulated around the four principles of international humanitarian law: truth, justice, reparation and guarantees of non-repetition. It is also an indisputable fact, ladies and gentlemen, that for forty years selective silence was a state policy. Silence and oblivion for the victims of the losing side, while the victims of the winners were repaired economically and morally and honored in all the peoples of Spain; a selective oblivion based on a biased interpretation of history. The story on the part of the victims was eliminated and that memory has become a ballast for several generations. As Mario Benedetti wrote, forgetfulness is full of memory. The victims of the dictatorship never forgot, because no one knows or can forget, even if they want, those memories. Neither helplessness nor pain are eliminated, and thousands of compatriots have clinged to the portraits of their parents, mothers, grandparents and grandmothers so as not to lose hope of finding them. I would like to point out that this project is also based on the concept of restorative justice, expressly guaranteeing the right to the truth of victims and of society in general, the right to investigate human rights violations and the right to judicial truth through a procedure of voluntary jurisdiction. It does not focus on punitive justice or economic redress, but it is true that, until December 2020, the last one completed, reparations have been made to more than 600 000 beneficiaries, for an overall amount of more than EUR 22 billion. Today more than 18,000 people still perceive economic reparation. Our law also advances the need for an audit, an inventory of seizures, spoiled property, paper money and sanctions imposed on individuals as a result of political responsibilities. In any case, we try with this rule to obtain moral reparation for all victims and the recognition of the truth. No one can fear the truth and the right to know it. As much as you are looking for it, ladies and gentlemen, you will not find in the text of this project an ideological account or a rereading of the past or a imposition by law of a single version or a single story; it is exactly the opposite. [[Rumors.-A honourable Member: No, what's going on!]] We want to create the channels and enable mechanisms for that complete truth, which for forty years was omitted, to be known. As the poet Marcos Ana has said so many times, it is a matter of passing the page, but having read me before. Finally, the duty of memory as a guarantee of non-repetition is a firm commitment to the pedagogy of never again, which has become an ethical imperative for advanced democracies like ours. Therefore, in line with the Constitution and international standards, the text provides for measures in the field of education and teacher training for pedagogical purposes. Consolidating a shared democratic memory, based on principles of international law, based on the democratic and constitutional values of our country is a pillar in any modern democracy. We understand that the recovery, safeguarding and dissemination of memory are essential in our democracy and we want to promote cohesion and solidarity between generations. It is a commitment today and it is a commitment to the future; it defends democracy and defends fundamental rights as a backbone of our Constitution. The project overcomes confrontation and deactivates hatred. ParlaMint-ES_2021-10-14-CD211014.u114	Thank you, Madam President. The theologians say that God does not even have the capacity to change the past, to make what happened never happened. For this reason, the despots of all time have always felt the temptation to rewrite history - that is to change the past and raise them above God -: from that Chinese emperor who burned all the ancient books because they did not contain his name, to Stalin, who made erase from the successive editions of the Soviet Encyclopedia the photos of the characters who were shooting, which disappear from the old photos of the revolution. Setting by decree a partisan interpretation of history and imposing on society as a whole using the coercive instruments of the State is the same as despots. In free countries, political power does not arrogate historical omniscience, but allows citizens to freely reach their own conclusions on historical issues from their own readings, their own experiences, conversations. And this is not just us, said, for example, among many, leftist historian Santos Juliá in 2006 - and I quote textually -: imposing a historical memory is the same as authoritarian regimes or totalitarian utopias. The draft law that we are debating today abolishes in practice freedom of association, as it threatens with the dissolution to those associations that do not share the historical vision of the Government, for example, the Fundación Santa Cruz del Valle de los Caídos; it abolishes in practice religious freedom, as it opens the door to the possible expulsion of the Benedictines from the valley, it suppresses freedom of assembly, freedom of chair and freedom of expression, since it turns the apologia of Francoism practically into a crime of thought. In addition, this law establishes channels for a constant historical indoctrination of the population or society, channels such as a four-year democratic memory plan - I do not know why they have not put in place a five-year plan, such as a wink at the good old Soviet times - Article 12; the impregnation of all school programmes, provided for in Article 44.1; the impregnation of teacher training plans, Article 44.2; the continuing training plans of the General Administration of the State, Article 47, and even the creation of a democratic memory council in Article 57, which recalls the sinister Ministry of Truth of 1984 in Orwell. [[applause]] Now 1984 is one of those dystopic novels that everyone quotes, but very few people have read; I would swear that especially in the left sector of the House very few have read it. [[Applauds]] A division that had already been overcome at the social level in families and in the streets some twenty years after the end of our Civil War and that was institutionally overcome in our Transition, a Transition in which both sides made generous concessions. The Franchise Courts disbanded themselves with remarkable heights of vision to make political reform possible in 1976 and also the left made concessions such as the acceptance of the monarchy or the red flag.
